# Project Design Tracker

This file is append-only. It records implementation status, changes, open gaps, decisions, documentation drift and verification runs.

## 1. Current status

The generated status matrix is in `project-design/STATUS.md`. Part-level status is the YAML `status` field in `project-design/parts/**`.

| 2026-11-05 | deploy.pipeline, deploy.storage, deploy.backup-daemon, testing.deploy-stress | Deploy engine optimisation and a live stress harness. Measured baseline: a no-op `--update` storage deploy took 4m53s (full update 5m14s) because PgBouncer 1.26 treats SIGTERM as "wait for all clients to disconnect" and core's pooled clients never leave, so every `systemctl restart pgbouncer` waited out the 90 s stop timeout, 3 times per deploy; PostgreSQL, Redis (flushing the cache) and the backup daemon were also restarted unconditionally. Fix: convergence (`deploy/lib/converge.ts`): `writeRemoteFile`/`sync` report change; PostgreSQL reload then restart only on `pending_restart`; PgBouncer reload / restart only for restart-only settings or after a PG restart, plus a `KillSignal=SIGINT` drop-in; Redis and the backup daemon restart only on change; packages installed only when missing (no `pacman -Sy`); `--force-restart`. Also: frontend builds are content-hash cached, parallel and overlapped with storage provisioning (`--rebuild`); ssh connection multiplexing; per-step and per-command timing report; credential values masked in all logs/errors (pgpass lines and URLs were previously printed in clear); co-located audit/log no longer rewrite the primary's conf/hba and the audit step no longer overwrites the shared cluster's `archive_command` (core owns the WAL stream); fixed an empty `ExecReload` ($MAINPID) in generated Arch units, replicas never applying config, and a bootstrap `psql` without `-p`. Found and fixed on the way: **audit-logs 500** - since commit `167aec5` pg_hba is `hostssl`-only but `AUDIT_DATABASE_URL`/`LOG_DATABASE_URL`/`DATABASE_REPLICA_URLS` carried no TLS parameter, so core's direct audit/log pools were rejected the first time a deploy applied it; now `?sslmode=no-verify` for deploy-managed clusters. The stress harness (`deploy/stress`, 11 scenarios) also found a Redis alias-unit bug (`redis.service` -> `valkey.service` refused by `systemctl enable`). | `deploy/deploy.ts`, `deploy/lib/{converge,frontendBuild,sshRunner,hostPlanner,osDetector}.ts`, `deploy/provisioners/*`, `deploy/stress/**`, `deploy/test/{converge,frontendBuild,stress-harness,redactSecrets,provisioners,fakeRails}.test.ts` | `project-design/parts/07-deploy/{04-pipeline,05-storage-provisioners,06-backup-daemon}.md`, `project-design/parts/08-testing/05-deploy-stress.md` | live runs on the dev box: storage no-op 4m53s -> 5 s; full no-op 23 s; stress suite results in the verification log |

| 2026-11-07 | core.ledger, core.workers, core.storage, core.data, core.api, edge.admin, admin-ui.routes, admin-ui.system, deploy.backup, testing.suites | **Sequenced per-shard ledger checkpoints, automatic checkpointing and shard scaling.** Research found the account-level checkpoint was unsound (a `created_at` time boundary stamped by the app process can miss a line that commits late; capture was O(all history) under a row lock that blocked postings; no shard attribution on `journal_lines`; one global shard count; deploy/env/DB disagreed on 16 vs 32). Schema v12 (`20261107090000_shard_sequenced_checkpoints`): `gl_account_shards.line_seq`, `journal_lines.shard_id/shard_seq` (+ `CHECK shard_seq > 0 NOT VALID`, covering index), new `gl_shard_checkpoints`, `gl_accounts.shard_count/shard_scaled_at`, trigger creating shard rows with every account, `operator_cases.subject_key`; genesis folds every account into shard 0 with the journal as truth and drops the account checkpoint columns. The poster now reserves each line's sequence in the shard upsert BEFORE inserting the lines (commit order = sequence order, gapless). Checkpoints are per shard (`CheckpointService.captureShard`: one snapshot statement proves gapless + parity, then a monotonic upsert; O(delta), lock-free); `LedgerCheckpointWorker` runs them; `LedgerShardScalerWorker` (dry run by default) + `applyShardWidth` widen hot accounts (also `POST /finance/gl-accounts/:id/shards/scale`). Wallet debits are pinned to shard 0 and `lockWalletLedgerBalance` locks only shard 0 (also used by the chargeback ceiling, which previously read every wallet line unlocked). Live balance is the shard sum only (journal fallback removed); invariant check is O(shards); gateway clearing aging is net movement by age in bounded SQL; wallet/merchant endpoints return natural-sign derived checkpoints. Admin: GL drawer per-shard checkpoint/status/width + Set shard count, Jobs & Outbox ledger automation tiles; restore drill and deploy gate use a per-shard proof; `STORAGE_SHARD_COUNT` is the initial width (1..256 everywhere). Benchmark: single-hot-shard write path about 25-30% slower (p50 1.4 to 1.8 ms; about 1,000 to 750 postings/s). Guide b03-09/b03-13/restore-drill text and Academy l-m2-2 updated. |

| 2026-11-07 | core.workers, core.ledger, core.data, deploy.storage, deploy.pipeline, testing.suites | **PgBouncer session advisory locks fixed (GAP-012).** Root cause: `PgAdvisoryLeaderElection` used `env.DATABASE_URL`, which points at PgBouncer's transaction pool (`pool_mode=transaction`); session locks then allowed two core nodes to acquire the same key reentrantly, silently disappeared on backend recycling, or leaked on unlock. New `LEADER_ELECTION_DATABASE_URL` + `PGBOUNCER_ENABLED` core env; deploy generates a `pgw_session` alias on the same PgBouncer port with `pool_mode=session` (or a direct `sslmode=no-verify` URL when PgBouncer is disabled) and proves a login through it. Core refuses to boot when the endpoint is missing or the transaction-pooled URL, runs a boot probe (a second client must not acquire the probe key; stable backend PID; unlock must succeed), and every singleton tick re-proves the backend PID + the `pg_locks` advisory row, failing closed otherwise. `ProductionWorkerRuntime` registers the expected singleton keys; `/health` exposes a sanitized `leader` payload. Defense in depth: migration `20261107120000_inbound_event_claims` (schema version 13) adds `inbound_events.claimed_at/claimed_by`; the inbox worker claims with a durable lease and finalizes only while it still owns the claim; KYB notice markers are conditional; the invariant case uses the shared `subject_key` dedupe. New live PgBouncer regression test proves the bug and the fix. | 2026-11-07 | core.ledger | **Cash-out vs payment-confirmation deadlock fixed (GAP-200).** A cash-out locked the wallet's shard-0 row for its balance check and then posted (locking a treasury shard) while a payment confirmation locks the treasury shard first and the wallet second: PostgreSQL aborted one with 40P01 (reproduced: 75 deadlocks in 120 concurrent operations when both touch the same two rows). `lockWalletLedgerBalance` now serializes wallet debits with a transaction advisory lock taken before any shard row is held; the cash-out additionally pre-locks the shard rows of the journal it will post in the global `(account, shard)` order (`LedgerPoster.lockShardsFor`, `lockShardsForLines`) before reading the balance; the chargeback ceiling check uses only the advisory lock. New `test/cashout-lock-order.test.ts`; `ledger-shard-sequence.test.ts` cash-out case updated to the new contract. Full core suite 23,148 pass / 3 known sandbox-DB failures. |

| 2026-11-07 | core.ledger, core.workers, core.api, edge.admin, admin-ui.routes, deploy.backup, testing.suites | **Shard repair (GAP-202 resolved): automated and manual.** A shard is a projection of the immutable journal, so a failed shard is repaired by recomputing the projection from the journal, never by posting a ledger entry (an adjusting entry changes the journal and leaves the shard just as wrong). `ShardRepair.ts` plans from ALL the shard's journal lines and decides RECOMPUTE (sequence intact: balance := journal sum, checkpoint := (sum, line_seq)) or REBASELINE (sequence damaged: balance := journal sum, line_seq := max(line_seq, highest sequence found), `baseline_seq` exempts the damaged history). `applyShardRepair` locks the shard row, re-plans under the lock, refuses if the plan hash changed, writes `gl_shard_repairs` + audit `GL_SHARD_REPAIRED` and annotates the (still open) operator case. Automatic: `LedgerCheckpointWorker` repairs only a persistent PARITY (>= 3 consecutive failures) with an intact sequence and a correction <= 100.0000 (`LEDGER_SHARD_AUTO_REPAIR_*`, on by default); never a GAP. Manual: `GET .../shards/:shardId/repair-plan`, `POST .../repair` (RECOMPUTE immediately, `finance:write`, reason, plan hash), `POST .../repair/request` (REBASELINE or any repair through Approvals as subject `GL_SHARD_REPAIR`, bound to the plan hash, maker != checker); GL drawer **Repair** dialog previews the correction first. Migration v14 (`baseline_seq`, `gl_shard_repairs`); restore drill and deploy gate honour the baseline. New tests: engine, API+approval, worker auto-repair, Playwright. |

| 2026-11-16 | core.object-storage, platform.sandbox, core.report-engine, deploy.pipeline | **Fixed shared-vault orphan sweep that deleted the running report ZIP.** Root cause: `ObjectStorageMaintenanceWorker` is a singleton per control-plane database. With `SANDBOX_ENABLED=true`, Live and Sandbox share the vault but have separate `storage_objects` tables; each plane's orphan sweep listed the whole vault and moved the other plane's blobs to `.trash/2026-11-15`. The report `gateway-performance-intelligence` ZIP (object `3e525d00-9bb9-40ea-9e28-9c32aa5999ba`) and 621 other active blobs (444 KYB, 169 reconciliation, 8 regulatory) were trashed while their rows stayed ACTIVE; the report failed `STORAGE_INTEGRITY_CORRUPTED: The encrypted blob ... is missing from the vault`. Fix: `OBJECT_STORAGE_ORPHAN_SWEEP_ENABLED` (default true; the deploy derives false when `SANDBOX_ENABLED`) and the SDK `orphanBlobSweepAllowed()` guard, with the Sandbox plane always skipping the destructive sweep; per-row GC, scrub, lock reconcile and trash retention stay enabled; a successful scrub resets `scrub_error_count`. Restored all 622 active blobs from `.trash`, ran 8 scrub batches (0 corrupted), and redeployed core + sandbox. | `core-back/lib/objectStorageSdk/index.ts`, `core-back/lib/env/index.ts`, `deploy/lib/hostPlanner.ts`, `core-back/test/objectStorageOrphanSweepGuard.test.ts`, `deploy/test/hostPlanner.test.ts` | `parts/01-core-back/13-object-storage.md` section 7 and 9, `parts/00-platform/05-sandbox-execution-plane.md` section 8 | core/deploy typechecks; guard and hostPlanner tests; live core redeploy 14/14, Verification OK, 23 PASS; report execute HTTP 200 with 16 datasets and `assetDataUrls`; 800 scrub verifications, 0 corrupted; report asset `scrub_error_count=0`; `.trash` empty |

| 2026-11-26 | deploy.codespaces (new part), deploy.topology, core.engine, core.seeder, fake-rails.backend, fake-rails.chaos, fake-rails.integration, testing.fixtures, design | **Codespaces environment, one-command launcher and live-stack accuracy remediation.** Added `.devcontainer/` (docker-compose with PostgreSQL 18, Bun/Node 22 image, post-create install, forwarded ports 5101-5104 and 4102 only), `scripts/codespaces-start.sh` (profiles `core`, `merchant-demo`, `full`; commands start/stop/status/urls/credentials/smoke/logs/reset-db; generated secrets and mTLS certificates stay in git-ignored `.local/codespaces/` and `core-back/certs/`; the core mTLS API is never forwarded; the only destructive command is `reset-db --yes-delete-dev-database`), `scripts/codespaces-smoke.ts` (real HTTP smoke test: health, mTLS refusal without a client certificate, unauthenticated refusal, a full simulated Edfali OTP payment on the Sandbox plane, wrong-OTP negative control) and a `codespaces` CI job. Running the full stack end to end exposed latent defects that are fixed here: (1) `scripts/deploy-localhost.sh` could not start core-back (missing `OBJECT_STORAGE_KEK_KEYRING`, `EXECUTION_PLANE`, plane-fence allowance for Fake Rails) and generated no policy-compliant development password; (2) `flowProvider` passed the Prisma `ContentType` enum name instead of a MIME string so `multipart/form-data` rail requests were never built, and `ApiConsumer` spread/JSON-stringified `FormData` into `{}` (new regression test `core-back/test/engine/multipart-and-content-type.test.ts`); (3) Fake Rails ignored `invoice_no`/`merchantRef` and omitted `env` in success bodies, which made the engine trusted-success facts check fail with `TRUSTED_SUCCESS_FACTS_FAILED`; (4) the Fake Rails seeder created no demo credential values so Sandbox rails failed with `API_KEY credential is missing`, and the widget URLs assumed `localhost` (now `FAKE_RAILS_BROWSER_BASE_URL`); (5) `create-dev-users` scoped the dev merchant to one application so Sandbox Studio showed nothing (`DEV_MERCHANT_UNSCOPED=true` opt-in); (6) `fake-rails-front` Vite `allowedHosts` honours `VITE_MY_TAILSCALE`/forwarded hosts; (7) Windows portability of `scripts/project-design/check.ts` (backslash glob paths) and a `.gitattributes` forcing LF so the generator and shell scripts survive `core.autocrlf`; (8) core-back dependency bump `zod` 4.6.7, `proxy-addr` 2.0.8, `body-parser` 2.4.0 (closes the critical proxy-addr IP-spoofing advisory and two highs); (9) the pre-existing CI step `git show --check --pretty=format: HEAD` is now quoted because the unquoted `: ` made `ci.yml` invalid YAML; (10) once `ci.yml` parsed for the first time its jobs exposed latent workflow defects, fixed here: cross-workspace imports need sibling `node_modules` (core-back job now installs admin-back, deploy, deploy/backup-daemon; deploy* backend jobs install core-back, deploy, backup-daemon, admin-back; the tests-workspace job installs admin/merchant/checkout/fake-rails-front) and the design whitespace check ran `git show` on a shallow PR merge commit (now `fetch-depth: 2` and `git diff --check HEAD^1 HEAD`). | `.devcontainer/**`, `.gitattributes`, `scripts/codespaces-start.sh`, `scripts/codespaces-smoke.ts`, `scripts/deploy-localhost.sh`, `scripts/project-design/check.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `core-back/engine/runtime/flowProvider.ts`, `core-back/engine/apiConsumer/ApiConsumer.ts`, `core-back/prisma/seedFakeRails.ts`, `core-back/scripts/create-dev-users.ts`, `fake-rails-back/api/rails/common.ts`, `fake-rails-front/vite.config.ts`, `core-back/package.json`, `core-back/bun.lock` | `project-design/parts/07-deploy/08-codespaces.md`, `parts/01-core-back/02-engine.md`, `parts/06-fake-rails/03-core-integration.md`, `parts/08-testing/02-fixtures-and-live-stack.md`, `README.md` | Local stack (Windows host + WSL PostgreSQL 18) ran a complete Sandbox payment lifecycle; | 2026-11-27 | admin-ui.system, testing.suites | **Fixed the merchant detail page tables failing to load every resource.** Root cause: the `rerender: true` mode of the shared `dc` helper evaluated the render function inside a `computed`; when the merchant page switched from its loading branch to the loaded branch, the computed re-evaluated during reactivity propagation (`walkDirty`) with no Vapor instance, so `createIf`/child components were mounted detached from the component tree and lost `provide`/`inject`. `usePgUiConfig()` returned the empty fallback, every `PgApiTable` threw before sending a request and rendered "Could not load data", and router/i18n injections were missing too. The render now runs inside its own `renderEffect` (owning instance active) and a second `renderEffect` applies the result to the `DynamicFragment`; splitting the two effects keeps mount-time writes out of the render pass's dependency set (tracking them together timed out the `PgDetailsDialog` package tests under the full run). Added the `dc rerender branch` regression (provider inheritance after a branch flip; fails with the old implementation), verified the live merchant page loads transactions, wallets, applications, cash-outs, disputes, adjustments, notifications, audit and field history, and redeployed the admin tier. | `packages/ui/src/lib/dc.ts`, `packages/ui/test/dc.test.tsx` | `project-design/parts/03-admin-front/00-ui-system.md` §19.9 | package 431 tests + typecheck; admin-front type-check, 37 unit tests and production build; live admin release `20261127175312` loads every merchant resource tab with `200` responses and no `usePgUiConfig`/`renderEffect` warnings; see §6 (2026-11-27 merchant detail table loading) |

| GAP-012 | P0 | core.workers | RESOLVED 2026-11-07. SKIP LOCKED claims run in a transaction; PollWorker and OutboxNotification lease rows (`claimLeaseSeconds`, default 120 s). Core refuses to boot when `PGBOUNCER_ENABLED=true` and the URL is missing/equal to `DATABASE_URL`; a boot probe (second client cannot acquire the probe key, stable backend PID) plus per-tick `pg_locks`/PID ownership checks fail closed. `InboundInboxWorker` has a durable `claimed_at`/`claimed_by` lease so a brief leadership overlap cannot process a webhook twice. | `core-back/lib/storageEngine/leaderElection.ts`, `core-back/lib/env/index.ts`, `core-back/index.ts`, `core-back/engine/workers/InboundInboxWorker.ts`, `deploy/provisioners/postgres18Provisioner.ts`, `deploy/lib/{configSchema,hostPlanner}.ts` | Keep `claimLeaseSeconds` above the longest per-row processing time; calibrate the session pool size when core nodes grow. |

| GAP-022 | P1 | deploy.backup | PARTIAL 2026-11-06. The backup daemon runs as `pgbackup` only in Barman mode; in WAL-G mode it must run as `postgres` (WAL-G reads the 0700 PGDATA and connects over the socket as `postgres`; verified on a live single-host deploy where `pgbackup` made every base backup fail with a peer-authentication error). The unit also needs `PrivateTmp=true`, otherwise the mTLS front cannot create its unix socket under `ProtectSystem=strict`. PostgreSQL provisioning enables `ssl` (self-signed per-cluster certificate) and emits `hostssl`-only `pg_hba` rules; PgBouncer uses `server_tls_sslmode = require`. | `deploy/provisioners/backupEngineProvisioner.ts`, `deploy/provisioners/postgres18Provisioner.ts` | Replace the self-signed certificate with a CA-signed one if `verify-full` is required. |

| GAP-052 | P1 | platform.security | RESOLVED 2026-11-08. Staff are created by invitation (no maker-set password or phone) and activation is blocked until the invitee completes it. Original finding: Staff user creation (`POST /internal/admin/users`) is a direct write: the maker chooses the new user's initial password and phone and sets role/authorities; the later `status: ACTIVE` approval shows the checker neither the phone nor that the maker set the password; there is no forced password change. A collusive or compromised maker can pass maker-checker. The MFA code goes to the maker-chosen phone. | `core-back/api/internal/admin.ts` `POST /users`, `PATCH /users/:id`; `admin-back/api/protected/users.ts` | Create users without a password and activate through an invitation flow (as merchants have), or show the checker the masked phone and a "password set by maker" flag and add `mustChangePassword`. |
| GAP-053 | P2 | platform.security | RESOLVED 2026-11-08. Composite `identifier|ip` bucket plus a large identifier ceiling in core and failures-only account limiters at both edges. Original finding: The login throttle keys the lock on the identifier alone (5 failures / 15 min lock) and counts every failure, so an anonymous caller can hold a known account locked (the check runs before the password, so the victim's correct password also gets 429); the per-IP bucket (30) locks everyone behind one NAT address. | `core-back/lib/auth/throttle.ts`, `admin.ts` login, `merchant.ts` login | Add a composite `identifier+ip` bucket (about 5) beside a much larger identifier-only ceiling, with backoff before a hard lock. |
| GAP-054 | P2 | edge.admin | RESOLVED 2026-11-08. Shared `uploadGuards` in both edges verify the session shape, limit per address and check Content-Length before multer. Original finding: Uploads are buffered in memory by multer before core validates the credential. merchant-back only checks that an Authorization header exists (10 uploads/min/IP, 10 MiB); admin-back has no upload limiter or Content-Length pre-check and only checks the token's shape (LAN-only). | `merchant-back/api/upload.ts`, `admin-back/api/upload.ts`, `admin-back/api/protected/index.ts` | Share merchant's `uploadGuards` with admin and verify the session shape/expiry before multer. |
| GAP-055 | P3 | checkout-ui.flow | RESOLVED 2026-11-08. Only the attempt `reference` is accepted and the inquiry is rate-limited per attempt (10 s, cluster-wide). Original finding: `GET|POST /return/:attemptToken` is unauthenticated and runs `engine.inquire` (an outbound gateway call that can move an attempt to `VERIFYING`) with only the per-IP limiter; core also accepts a provider-controlled `gatewayReference` as the token. The attempt id is NOT returned (refuted); the internal `reference` is, in the redirect. | `checkout-back/api/public/return.ts`, `core-back/api/internal/checkout.ts:1578` | Accept only `reference`, and skip `inquire` when the attempt was verified in the last N seconds. Check every rail's return parameters first. |
| GAP-056 | P1 | deploy.pipeline | PARTIAL 2026-11-08. Auto-mode probe, `PG_RAM_GB` preflight, step swap and guide fixed; not run against a real bare multi-host lab. Original finding: `DEPLOY_MODE=auto` never probes a host (it acts as update mode), so a bare host fails at rsync/`bun install`, and on an edge host that is after core was migrated and switched; no resource preflight (PostgreSQL is sized for 32 GB unless `PG_RAM_GB` is set); verification (step 12) runs after the up-to-60-minute seed (step 11); `deploy/DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE.md` still says auto = fresh when the remote dir is absent. | `deploy/deploy.ts:976`, `deploy/provisioners/postgres18Provisioner.ts:183` | Per-host read-only bootstrap probe in step 4 (`bootstrapGaps()` pure function), `PG_RAM_GB` warning/probe, swap steps 11 and 12. |
| GAP-057 | P1 | deploy.storage | PARTIAL 2026-11-08. App/owner role split, per-client PgBouncer auth and client TLS implemented and validated end to end on a single TLS PostgreSQL 18 + real PgBouncer; certificate verification (`verify-ca`) and a multi-host lab run remain. Original finding: The deployed core connects as the table owner (`PG_DB_USER`), so the `pgw_app` role never applies; the owner is still blocked by the append-only triggers (UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE), but could drop them. PgBouncer's client leg has no TLS (`server_tls_sslmode = require` covers only PgBouncer -> PostgreSQL on loopback, unverified), direct links use `sslmode=no-verify`, and `DATABASE_URL` carries no `sslmode` (rejected by the hostssl-only `pg_hba` when PgBouncer is off). | `deploy/lib/configSchema.ts`, `deploy/provisioners/postgres18Provisioner.ts`, `deploy/lib/hostPlanner.ts` | Separate owner and login roles (app logs in as a member of `pgw_app`), `client_tls_*` on PgBouncer and `verify-ca` URLs; needs a live multi-host test. |
| GAP-058 | P2 | deploy.storage | PARTIAL 2026-11-08. `volatile-ttl` + AOF, atomic `SET PX` and a durable `session_revocations` table fallback; no live Redis restart drill. Original finding: The session revoke list shares a Redis with `maxmemory-policy allkeys-lru` and an hourly RDB only: a revoke flag can be evicted or lost on restart, and the fail-open fallback then honours it only on the node that processed the revoke. | `deploy/provisioners/redisProvisioner.ts:33`, `core-back/lib/auth/sharedState.ts` | `volatile-lru` plus AOF (every other key must carry a TTL first) or a dedicated `noeviction` Redis; write revokes with one atomic `SET ... PX`. |
| GAP-059 | P2 | fake-rails.protocol | RESOLVED 2026-11-08. Webhook targets are allowlisted at accept and send time and redirects are not followed. Only the peer allowlist stands in front. | `fake-rails-back/api/rails/common.ts:219`, `engine/webhooks/WebhookDispatcher.ts:276` | Accept only the `CORE_INBOUND_BASE_URL` host or an env allowlist, `redirect: "manual"`, validate again at send time. |

| GAP-062 | P2 | core.data | PARTIAL 2026-11-08. Runbook for the maintenance window written (`core.data` section 12); the applied migration is not edited and no deploy-time guard exists yet. Original finding: Migration `20261107090000_shard_sequenced_checkpoints` is not safe on a live populated database: the `shard_id` CHECK is not `NOT VALID` (ACCESS EXCLUSIVE scan of every partition), the `journal_lines` index is built non-concurrently on the partitioned parent, the genesis aggregate runs unlocked and can miss postings committed before the shard rewrite, there is no `lock_timeout`, and the previous binary's inserts fail once `shard_seq` is NOT NULL. It is already applied everywhere it was tried, so it was not edited. | `core-back/prisma/migrations/20261107090000_shard_sequenced_checkpoints/migration.sql` | Treat it as a maintenance-window migration (stop postings, deploy the new binary together); a safer variant needs a new pre-step migration. |

Original finding: Throughput toward 15,000 TPS is not addressed: 5-6 database round trips per ledger posting and about 15-18 per confirmation (mostly Prisma overhead), treasury/income hot rows whose shard locks are held through commit, and a shard scaler that reacts too slowly to a step load. The reviewer's 4-9x estimate for a single combined SQL statement per posting is not measured. | `core-back/engine/ledger/LedgerPoster.ts`, `lib/storageEngine/ledgerShardWriter.ts`, `engine/ledger/ShardScaler.ts` | Benchmark first (`scripts/run-benchmarks.sh`); combined statement with cached account lookups; pre-size hot accounts wider; shorter lock hold. |

Measured 2026-11-06 on one 3.7 GB WSL VM that also hosts PostgreSQL: peak about 900 postings/s at 16-32 concurrent posters, about 600/s at 256 (a few 5 s interactive-transaction timeouts, rolled back, no drift). Re-checked 2026-11-09 on master `72686ec`: 877 posts/s at 8 posters, 771 at 32, all five checks PASS, 0 errors.
